<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove the spherical fermionic Laughlin spectral-gap conjecture for the full <i>V</i><sub>1</sub> interaction. With coefficient one for each pair projector, the gap above the Laughlin state at filling 1/3 on the round sphere is at least 1/25 for all sufficiently large systems. More generally, $`H_Q^2\ge\gamma H_Q`$ for some fixed $`\gamma\gt 1/25`$ on the entire lowest-Landau-level Fock space for all sufficiently large flux <i>Q</i>, independently of particle number.

- **C1** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-fock)): For every $0<\gamma<\gamma_*$, there is an integer $Q_\gamma$ such that for every integer $Q\ge Q_\gamma$, $$\label{eq:architecture} H_Q^2\ge\gamma H_Q\qquad\text{on }\mathcal F_Q.$$ The threshold $Q_\gamma$ is independent of particle number.
  - Explanation: Bounds the positive spectrum on the entire spherical Fock space for sufficiently large flux, uniformly in particle number.
- **C2** (bound, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): There is an integer $N_0\ge2$ such that, for every $N\ge N_0$ and $Q=3(N-1)$, $$\label{eq:main} H_{N,Q}\ge\frac1{25}\bigl(I-P_{\mathrm L,N}\bigr) \qquad\text{on }\bigwedge^N U_Q,$$ where $P_{\mathrm L,N}$ projects onto the line spanned by $$\Psi_{\mathrm L,N}=\prod_{i<j}(u_iv_j-u_jv_i)^3.$$
  - Explanation: Bounds the energy above the unique spherical Laughlin zero mode at Laughlin flux for sufficiently large particle number.
- **C3** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-square)): On $\mathcal F_Q$, $$\label{eq:square} H^2=H+\mathcal L_3\!\left(W_3(W_3^\dagger W_3-I)W_3^\dagger\right) +\mathcal L_4(W_4W_4^\dagger).$$
  - Explanation: Separates the square of the interaction into normal-ordered pair, three-body, and four-body contributions.
- **C4** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-pair)): The coefficient of $e_i\wedge e_j$ in $v_p$, for $i<j$, vanishes unless $i+j=p+1$, and otherwise equals $$\label{eq:pair} v_p(i,j)=(i-j)\sqrt{\frac{(Q)_i(Q)_j\,p!}{Q(2Q-2)_p\,i!j!}}.$$ For fixed $p,i,j$, its limit is $$\label{eq:pairstar} v_p^*(i,j)=(i-j)\sqrt{\frac{p!}{2^p i!j!}}\,\mathbf1_{i+j=p+1}.$$
  - Explanation: Determines normalized spherical pair coefficients and their fixed-label limits.
- **C5** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-q)): $$\label{eq:q} W_3^\dagger W_3-I=\sum_{z=0}^Qq_z(Q)R_{3,z},\qquad q_z(Q)=(-1)^z\frac{(Q)_z}{(2Q-2)_z} \left(3z-1-\frac{z(z+1)}Q\right).$$ In particular $W_3R_{3,z}=0$ for $z=0,1,3$ whenever these indices are allowed. For fixed $z$, $$\label{eq:qlimit} q_z(Q)\longrightarrow (3z-1)(-1/2)^z.$$
  - Explanation: Determines the three-body Gram spectrum, its null sectors, and its fixed-deficit limit.
- **C6** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-coupling)): Suppose $j_1,j_2\to\infty$ and $j_2/(j_1+j_2)\to u^2$, where $0<u<1$. Put $v=\sqrt{1-u^2}$. For fixed integers $0\le z\le T$, the normalized coupled vector of spin $j_1+j_2-z$ at total lowering deficit $T$ can be chosen with real coefficients converging to the coefficients $s^{(u)}_{z,T,p}$ of $$\label{eq:coupling} \frac{(uX-vY)^z(vX+uY)^{T-z}}{\sqrt{z!(T-z)!}}.$$ Descendants in every copy are obtained by normalized lowering, so the matching of bases between equal-spin copies is intertwining.
  - Explanation: Identifies fixed-deficit coupling limits while retaining intertwining between equivalent spin copies.
- **C7** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-compression)): Let $W$ be a linear map between finite-dimensional Hilbert spaces, $G=W^\dagger W$, and let $C$ be self-adjoint on the domain of $W$. Then $$GCG\ge0\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad WCW^\dagger\ge0.$$ On $\mathcal F_{\mathrm{loc}}$, for any fixed finite collection $A_b=c_kc_jB(v_p^*)$ with $0\le p\le23$ and $0\le j,k\le24$, $$\label{eq:annihilatorbound} \sum_b\lVert A_b\psi\rVert^2\le C_0\langle \psi,h_*\psi\rangle, \qquad h_*:=\sum_{p=0}^{23}B(v_p^*)^\dagger B(v_p^*),$$ where $C_0$ depends only on the collection.
  - Explanation: Tests positivity after compression without assuming an invertible Gram matrix and bounds local annihilators by pair energy.
- **C8** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-transfer)): For $Q\ge24$ and $1\le D\le23$, put $$C_D^Q=(d_D/d_a)\chi-E_D^Q+\varepsilon I, \qquad h_Q=\sum_{p=0}^{23}B_p^\dagger B_p.$$ Assuming <a href="#eq:4certificate" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:4certificate">[eq:4certificate]</a>, there is a scalar $\rho_Q\ge0$, independent of $D$ and tending to zero as $Q\to\infty$, such that $$\label{eq:relative} \sum_{r,s\in\mathcal R_D}(C_D^Q)_{rs} B(w_{Dr}^Q)^\dagger B(w_{Ds}^Q) \ge-\rho_Qh_Q$$ on the entire Fock space $\mathcal F_Q$.
  - Explanation: Transfers the limiting certificate to finite spheres through an error controlled by pair energy, conditional on the certificate.
- **C9** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-a4)): For the coefficients in Appendix <a href="#app:certificate" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="app:certificate">8</a>, $$\label{eq:A4bound} A_4\le\mathcal L_4(W_4W_4^\dagger)+(1222\varepsilon+o(1))H, \qquad Q\to\infty,$$ uniformly on $\mathcal F_Q$.
  - Explanation: Pays the four-body comparison allowance in pair energy with an error uniform across particle sectors.
- **C10** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-tail)): For sufficiently large $Q$, $$\label{eq:tailbound} \mathcal L_3\left(W_3\sum_{z=16}^Qq_z(Q)R_{3,z}W_3^\dagger\right) \ge-\delta_QH,$$ where $$\label{eq:delta} \delta_Q=\sum_{\substack{17\le z\le Q\\z\text{ odd}}} \frac{d_{3,z}}{d_a}\|q_z(Q)\|(z+1),\qquad \delta_Q\longrightarrow\frac{61}{4096}.$$
  - Explanation: Controls the remaining three-body sectors by a summable relative error and evaluates its limit.
- **C11** (classification, [source](#stmt-lem-kernel)): For $N\ge2$ and $Q=3(N-1)$, the kernel of $H_{N,Q}$ is the line spanned by $\Psi_{\mathrm L,N}$.
  - Explanation: Identifies the spherical Laughlin-flux kernel through divisibility by cubed spinor brackets.
- **C12** (bound, [source](#stmt-cor-charge-neutral)): For $N\ge2$, fix $Q_N=3(N-1)$ and write $E_0(n;Q_N)=\min\operatorname{spec}H_{n,Q_N}$ for the coefficient-one interaction <a href="#eq:physicalH" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:physicalH">[eq:physicalH]</a>. Let $\operatorname{Gap}_N$ be the first positive eigenvalue of $H_{N,Q_N}$, its neutral gap. The particle-number charge gap at this same flux, $$\Delta_N=E_0(N+1;Q_N)+E_0(N-1;Q_N)-2E_0(N;Q_N),$$ satisfies $$\Delta_N=E_0(N+1;Q_N) \ge\frac{N}{N-1}\operatorname{Gap}_N>0.$$ In particular, $\Delta_N\ge N/[25(N-1)]$ for $N\ge N_0$, with $N_0$ as in Corollary <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">2</a>.
  - Explanation: Relates the charge gap to the neutral gap at the same Laughlin flux and derives an asymptotic lower bound.
- **C13** (bound, [source](#stmt-cor-plane)): For every $N\ge2$, the untruncated planar Hamiltonian satisfies $$(H_N^{\mathrm{pl}})^2\ge\gamma_* H_N^{\mathrm{pl}},$$ where $\gamma_*$ is the constant in Theorem <a href="#thm:fock" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:fock">1</a>. Equivalently, if $P_{0,N}^{\mathrm{pl}}$ projects onto its kernel, then $H_N^{\mathrm{pl}}\ge\gamma_*(I-P_{0,N}^{\mathrm{pl}})$. The constant is independent of both particle number and total angular momentum. In particular, the case $\ell=3$ of the spectral gap conjecture in (Rougerie 2018--2019, Conjecture A.1) [[Rougerie](#ref-Rougerie)] holds, in its pair-projector normalization.
  - Explanation: Establishes the endpoint planar bound for the untruncated interaction independently of particle number and total angular momentum.

        "exact_quote": "Suppose $j_1,j_2\\to\\infty$ and $j_2/(j_1+j_2)\\to u^2$, where $0<u<1$. Put $v=\\sqrt{1-u^2}$.         "exact_quote": "For $N\\ge2$, fix $Q_N=3(N-1)$ and write $E_0(n;Q_N)=\\min\\operatorname{spec}H_{n,Q_N}$ for the coefficient-one interaction <a href=\"#eq:physicalH\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:physicalH\">[eq:physicalH]</a>. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **spherical one-particle space** ([source](#eq:physicalH)): For an integer $Q\ge1$, let $U_Q$ be the spin-$Q/2$ representation of $SU(2)$, equipped with its invariant inner product.
- **spherical interaction** ([source](#eq:physicalH)): For $2\le N\le Q+1$, define $$\label{eq:physicalH} H_{N,Q}=\left.\sum_{1\le i<j\le N}P_{ij}^{(1)} \right\|_{\bigwedge^N U_Q},$$ where $P_{ij}^{(1)}$ is the orthogonal projector onto pair spin $Q-1$ in tensor positions $i,j$.
- **Fock interaction and gap constant** ([source](#pm-h-3)): Set $H_{0,Q}=H_{1,Q}=0$, and write $$\mathcal F_Q=\bigoplus_{N=0}^{Q+1}\bigwedge^N U_Q,\qquad H_Q=\bigoplus_{N=0}^{Q+1}H_{N,Q},\qquad \gamma_*=\frac{4616733319001}{10^{14}}> \frac1{25}.$$
- **pair contraction** ([source](#sec:algebra)): On $$\mathcal F_Q=\bigoplus_{n=0}^{Q+1}\bigwedge^n U_Q,$$ let $B(v)$ be the adjoint of left wedge multiplication by $v$.
- **positive lift** ([source](#sec:algebra)): For a matrix on $\bigwedge^k U_Q$, define its lift by linear extension of $$\label{eq:lift} \mathcal L_k(\lvert v\rangle\langle w\rvert)=B(v)^\dagger B(w).$$
- **wedge maps** ([source](#sec:algebra)): Define intertwining wedge maps $$W_3:V_Q\otimes U_Q\longrightarrow\bigwedge^3U_Q, \qquad W_4:V_Q\otimes V_Q\longrightarrow\bigwedge^4U_Q,$$ by $W_3(v\otimes u)=v\wedge u$ and $W_4(v\otimes w)=v\wedge w$.
- **local exterior algebra** ([source](#sec:transfer)): For the local argument, let $\mathcal F_{\mathrm{loc}}$ be the exterior algebra of $\mathbb C^{25}$ with orthonormal basis $e_0,\ldots,e_{24}$.
- **Bargmann space** ([source](#sec:plane)): Let $\mathcal B$ be the Bargmann space of entire functions with norm $$\\|f\\|_{\mathcal B}^2=\frac1\pi\int_{\mathbb C}\|f(z)\|^2e^{-\|z\|^2}\,d^2z, \qquad e_j(z)=\frac{z^j}{\sqrt{j!}}.$$
- **planar interaction** ([source](#sec:plane)): Let $P^{(1)}$ be the orthogonal projector onto relative degree one, with no restriction on the degree in $Z$, and put $$H_N^{\mathrm{pl}}=\left.\sum_{1\le i<j\le N}P_{ij}^{(1)} \right\|_{\bigwedge^N\mathcal B}.$$

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **square** [claim](#stmt-lem-square) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-square): Normal ordering and restrictions to successive particle sectors. -> Exact body decomposition.
- **spin** [claim](#stmt-lem-q) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-q): Raising equations and swap compression. -> Three-body Gram eigenvalues and fixed-deficit limits.
- **certificate** [claim](#stmt-auto-remark-1) / [proof](#app:certificate): Exact rational trace formulas and characteristic-coefficient positivity. -> Finite limiting matrix inequalities.
- **transfer** [claim](#stmt-lem-transfer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-transfer): Diagonal conjugation and pair-energy control before rotation averaging. -> Uniform relative error on all particle sectors.
- **four-body** [claim](#stmt-prop-a4) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-a4): Bessel contraction before wedging and rotation averaging. -> Four-body comparison with allowance cost.
- **tail** [claim](#stmt-lem-tail) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-tail): Summable domination and dominated convergence. -> Relative tail bound and rational limiting coefficient.
- **fock-gap** [claim](#stmt-thm-fock) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-tail-2): Combine positive squares and relative comparison margins. -> Asymptotic Fock-space inequality.
- **kernel** [claim](#stmt-lem-kernel) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-kernel): Polynomial divisibility and unique factorization. -> Unique Laughlin-flux zero mode.
- **spherical-gap** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-kernel-2): Spectral theorem at Laughlin flux. -> Ground-state gap for sufficiently large particle number.
- **charge-gap** [claim](#stmt-cor-charge-neutral) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-charge-neutral): Recursive sector inequality and ground-space overlap bound. -> Charge versus neutral gap at fixed flux.
- **planar-gap** [claim](#stmt-cor-plane) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-plane): Norm convergence at fixed particle number and degree, then closedness of the positive cone. -> Planar endpoint inequality on the Hilbert direct sum.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **I_COUPLING**: The coupling coefficient is given by the finite signed binomial sum, with zero coefficients outside the stated support.
- **I_NORMALIZATION**: Each unordered pair projector has coefficient one without particle-number or flux rescaling.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **A_THRESHOLD**: Apply the spherical endpoint bound at arbitrary finite flux. -> An existential convergence threshold does not certify a selected finite sphere.
- **A_CERTIFICATE**: Treat coupling-coefficient agreement as a complete spectral-gap certificate. -> The row-dependent traces and four-body matrices have not been checked by coupling arithmetic alone.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `algorithmic`. The finite binomial coupling sum admits exact integer evaluation. This implementation checks that arithmetic, while the row-dependent positivity comparisons remain separate.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON list of integer quadruples [c,z,T,p].
# PRECONDITIONS: c in {1,2}, 0 <= T <= 15, -1 <= z,p <= T+1; at most 20000 quadruples.
# OUTPUTS: JSON list of exact integer coupling coefficients, in input order.
# TIME: O(number of inputs times T) exact integer arithmetic operations.
# SPACE: O(number of inputs) integers, excluding input storage.
# ARITHMETIC: python-flint fmpz exact integers; no floating point.
# FAILURE: invalid input raises ValueError; coefficient is zero outside [0,T].
import json, sys
from flint import fmpz
def coefficient(c,z,T,p):
    if not 0 <= z <= T or not 0 <= p <= T:
        return 0
    total = fmpz(0)
    for h in range(max(0,p+z-T), min(p,z)+1):
        total += (-1)**(z-h) * fmpz(z).bin_uiui(z,h) * fmpz(T-z).bin_uiui(T-z,p-h) * c**(p-h)
    return int(total)
data = json.loads(sys.stdin.read())
if not isinstance(data,list) or len(data)>20000:
    raise ValueError("invalid batch")
for row in data:
    if not isinstance(row,list) or len(row)!=4 or any(type(a) is not int for a in row):
        raise ValueError("integer quadruple required")
    c,z,T,p = row
    if c not in (1,2) or not 0<=T<=15 or not -1<=z<=T+1 or not -1<=p<=T+1:
        raise ValueError("outside admitted input contract")
print(json.dumps([coefficient(*row) for row in data],separators=(",",":")))
```

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The operator inequalities provide a specification for checking a faithful Hamiltonian representation, while the finite certificate offers exact arithmetic checks. |
| games_simulation | True | weak | adaptable | inferred | A simulation of the specified quantum Hall Hamiltonian can use the spectral inequalities as conditional model diagnostics. The paper supplies no time-evolution or game algorithm. |
| tools | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | A dedicated checker can evaluate the rational matrix formulas and characteristic coefficients. The required coefficient input must be supplied before the full certificate is reproducible. |
| algorithms | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The finite rational recurrence specifies a positivity decision procedure for the real symmetric matrices constructed in the appendix. |
| data_structures | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The rational matrices and diagonal congruence provide a representation for comparison on wedged images even when the limiting Gram matrix has a kernel. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper specifies mathematical comparisons and rational arithmetic, but no accelerator architecture or hardware execution contract. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The certificate establishes Hamiltonian matrix positivity; no cryptographic construction or security property is supplied. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The comparison coefficients support exact spectral inequalities; the paper gives no learning model, training method, or prediction guarantee. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The explicit positive-square certificate verifies fixed operator inequalities; the paper supplies no optimisation solver or operations-research contract. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The uniformity concerns particle sectors of a Hamiltonian, not distributed execution, agreement, or consistency. |
| other (Mathematical physics of quantum Hall spectral gaps) | True | strong | adaptable | inferred | The results directly concern rigorous spectral analysis of the spherical and planar fermionic quantum Hall parent Hamiltonians. Their use requires the stated geometry, normalization, and sector conditions. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **L-normalization** (hypothesis): The spherical interaction uses orthogonal pair projectors onto relative angular momentum one, with coefficient one for every unordered pair and no particle-number or flux rescaling. Blocked use: ['Applying the same numerical bound to differently weighted pair interactions without an additional comparison.']
- **L-spherical-threshold** (nonconstructive): The spherical flux and particle-number thresholds are existential; the proof supplies neither an explicit flux threshold nor the endpoint coefficient on finite spheres. Blocked use: ['Certifying a chosen finite spherical system solely from the asymptotic theorem.', 'Claiming the spherical endpoint coefficient without a separate estimate.']
- **L-sector-gap** (edge_case): In a particle sector without a zero mode, the quadratic inequality bounds energy away from zero but does not bound the spacing between the lowest eigenvalues. Blocked use: ['Interpreting a positive-sector energy floor as a gap above a positive-energy sector ground state.']
- **L-laughlin-flux** (hypothesis): The spherical unique-kernel conclusion is stated for $N\ge2$ and $Q=3(N-1)$. Blocked use: ['Assuming the kernel remains a line at a different flux from the stated Laughlin flux.']
- **L-relative-transfer** (approximation): Uniform transfer requires errors controlled by pair energy and vanishing on zero modes; a small norm error on the growing many-particle space does not suffice. Blocked use: ['Replacing the relative transfer estimate with an unqualified absolute norm approximation.']
- **L-singular-gram** (edge_case): Limiting Gram matrices may have kernels, so continuity of their ranges cannot be assumed when transferring positivity. Blocked use: ['Inverting a limiting Gram matrix or assuming its wedged columns are independent.']
- **L-planar-model** (hypothesis): The planar consequence concerns the untruncated fermionic Bargmann-space interaction with orthogonal relative-degree-one pair projectors and unrestricted center-of-mass degree. Blocked use: ['Transferring the planar conclusion to a truncated interaction or a different relative pair degree without an additional result.']
- **L-planar-limit** (approximation): The sphere-to-plane proof passes to the flux limit on each fixed particle-number and homogeneous-degree block, then takes the coefficient to its endpoint. Blocked use: ['Treating the proof as a quantitative simultaneous convergence rate for growing particle number and degree.']
- **L-row-data-absent** (unknown): ABSENT: the integer coefficient rows are missing from the supplied row-data block, so the complete finite matrix certificate cannot be independently reproduced from the supplied text. Blocked use: ['Treating the printed margin and sign tables as independently recomputed certificate outputs.', 'Replacing the absent coefficient rows with zero rows or inferred values.']
- **L-finite-certificate-scope** (formal_scope): The finite arithmetic certificate checks fixed spin-matrix inequalities; it is not a many-particle finite-size spectral computation. Blocked use: ['Reporting checks of an auxiliary coefficient formula as certification of the full spectral-gap theorem.', 'Using finite-size numerical extrapolation as the certificate described here.']
- **L-variational-energy** (counterexample_scope): A variational excitation energy is an upper bound and cannot establish the required lower bound for every state orthogonal to the zero modes. Blocked use: ['Certifying the spectral lower bound from a selected variational excitation.']
- **L-charge-flux** (hypothesis): The charge-versus-neutral comparison is taken at the same Laughlin flux for the adjacent particle sectors. Blocked use: ['Using the stated charge comparison for sectors with independently changed flux or interaction normalization.']

>
> ## Scope
>
> The linked formalization proves the uniform unperturbed gap estimate used in the paper's stability argument for the fermionic Laughlin state at filling $1/3$ on the sphere. At flux $q=3(N-1)$ and all sufficiently large particle numbers $N$, every antisymmetric state has $V_1$ energy at least $1/25$ times its squared distance from the Laughlin ground-state line.
>
> This selected statement is the unperturbed Fock-space inequality. Stability under projected one-body potentials and uniqueness of the perturbed ground state are outside it.
>
> The Laughlin spectral-gap problem asks for a positive gap that remains uniform as the system grows. The formalization includes the finite spherical $V_1$ bound of $1/100$ above the Laughlin state at flux $3(N-1)$ for all sufficiently large particle numbers $N$. It also proves the stronger Fock-space inequality $H_Q^2\ge\gamma H_Q$ for every fixed $0<\gamma<\gamma_*$ and all sufficiently large fluxes $Q$, independently of particle number, where $\gamma_*=4616733319001/10^{14}>1/25$.
>
> For the untruncated planar model, the formalization proves the corresponding inequality at the endpoint $\gamma_*$ on every homogeneous particle sector. These statements use coefficient one for each pair projector.

Comparator source `LaughlinFock.lean` (1995 bytes, sha256 `bf325bca1126710ec1fec832aad8135036848a3ddc1ebbcf5cdfea14107b84bf`), verbatim:

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

/-! The spherical V₁ Hamiltonian inequality on the full fermionic Fock space. -/

noncomputable section

namespace Laughlin

noncomputable def pairCoefficient (Q p : ℕ) (x y : Fin (Q+1)) : ℝ :=
  if x.val + y.val = p+1 then
    ((x.val : ℝ) - (y.val : ℝ)) *
      Real.sqrt (((Q.descFactorial x.val : ℝ) * (Q.descFactorial y.val : ℝ) *
        (p.factorial : ℝ)) /
        ((Q : ℝ) * ((2*Q-2).descFactorial p : ℝ) *
          (x.val.factorial : ℝ) * (y.val.factorial : ℝ))) / Real.sqrt 2
  else 0

end Laughlin

run_cmd Lean.modifyEnv fun environment => Lean.Meta.auxLemmasExt.setState environment {}

namespace LaughlinFock

open scoped BigOperators Matrix ComplexConjugate ComplexOrder

abbrev Orbital (Q : ℕ) := Fin (Q + 1)
abbrev Occupation (Q : ℕ) := Finset (Orbital Q)
abbrev FockMatrix (Q : ℕ) := Matrix (Occupation Q) (Occupation Q) ℂ

def tensorPairCoefficient (Q p : ℕ) (i j : Orbital Q) : ℝ :=
  _root_.OAI.Laughlin.pairCoefficient Q p i j

def pairCoefficient (Q p : ℕ) (i j : Orbital Q) : ℝ :=
  Real.sqrt 2 * tensorPairCoefficient Q p i j

def annihilator (Q : ℕ) (j : Orbital Q) : FockMatrix Q := fun A B =>
  if j ∈ B ∧ A = B.erase j then
    (-1 : ℂ) ^ (B.filter (fun i => i < j)).card
  else 0

def pairAnnihilator (Q p : ℕ) : FockMatrix Q :=
  ∑ i : Orbital Q, ∑ j : Orbital Q,
    if i < j then
      (pairCoefficient Q p i j : ℂ) • (annihilator Q j * annihilator Q i)
    else 0

def hamiltonian (Q : ℕ) : FockMatrix Q :=
  ∑ p ∈ Finset.range (2 * Q - 1),
    (pairAnnihilator Q p)ᴴ * pairAnnihilator Q p

def gammaStar : ℝ := 4616733319001 / 10 ^ 14

def FockInequality (Q : ℕ) (γ : ℝ) : Prop :=
  (hamiltonian Q * hamiltonian Q - γ • hamiltonian Q).PosSemidef

theorem thm_fock (γ : ℝ) (hγ : 0 < γ) (hγStar : γ < gammaStar) :
    ∃ Qγ : ℤ, 1 ≤ Qγ ∧ ∀ Q : ℤ, Qγ ≤ Q → FockInequality Q.toNat γ := by
  sorry

end LaughlinFock

end

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove the spherical fermionic Laughlin spectral-gap conjecture for the full <i>V</i><sub>1</sub> interaction. With coefficient one for each pair projector, the gap above the Laughlin state at filling 1/3 on the round sphere is at least 1/25 for all sufficiently large systems. More generally, $`H_Q^2\ge\gamma H_Q`$ for some fixed $`\gamma\gt 1/25`$ on the entire lowest-Landau-level Fock space for all sufficiently large flux <i>Q</i>, independently of particle number.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>
#### Statements index

| anchor | kind | label | number | first sentence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [stmt-thm-fock](#stmt-thm-fock) | theorem | `thm:fock` | 1 | For every $0<\gamma<\gamma_*$, there is an integer $Q_\gamma$ such that for every integer $Q\ge Q_\gamma$, |
| [stmt-thm-main](#stmt-thm-main) | corollary | `thm:main` | 2 | There is an integer $N_0\ge2$ such that, for every $N\ge N_0$ and $Q=3(N-1)$, |
| [stmt-lem-square](#stmt-lem-square) | lemma | `lem:square` | 3 | On $\mathcal F_Q$, |
| [stmt-lem-pair](#stmt-lem-pair) | lemma | `lem:pair` | 4 | The coefficient of $e_i\wedge e_j$ in $v_p$, for |
| [stmt-lem-q](#stmt-lem-q) | lemma | `lem:q` | 5 |  |
| [stmt-lem-coupling](#stmt-lem-coupling) | lemma | `lem:coupling` | 6 | Suppose $j_1,j_2\to\infty$ and $j_2/(j_1+j_2)\to u^2$, where $0<u<1$. |
| [stmt-lem-compression](#stmt-lem-compression) | lemma | `lem:compression` | 7 | Let $W$ be a linear map between finite-dimensional Hilbert spaces, $G=W^\dagger W$, and let $C$ be self-adjoint on the domain of $W$. |
| [stmt-lem-transfer](#stmt-lem-transfer) | lemma | `lem:transfer` | 8 | For $Q\ge24$ and $1\le D\le23$, put |
| [stmt-prop-a4](#stmt-prop-a4) | proposition | `prop:A4` | 9 | For the coefficients in Appendix 8, |
| [stmt-lem-tail](#stmt-lem-tail) | lemma | `lem:tail` | 10 | For sufficiently large $Q$, |
| [stmt-lem-kernel](#stmt-lem-kernel) | lemma | `lem:kernel` | 11 | For $N\ge2$ and $Q=3(N-1)$, the kernel of $H_{N,Q}$ is the line spanned by $\Psi_{\mathrm L,N}$.* |
| [stmt-cor-charge-neutral](#stmt-cor-charge-neutral) | corollary | `cor:charge-neutral` | 12 | For $N\ge2$, fix $Q_N=3(N-1)$ and write $E_0(n;Q_N)=\min\operatorname{spec}H_{n,Q_N}$ for the coefficient-one interaction [eq:physicalH]. |
| [stmt-cor-plane](#stmt-cor-plane) | corollary | `cor:plane` | 13 | For every $N\ge2$, the untruncated planar Hamiltonian satisfies $$(H_N^{\mathrm{pl}})^2\ge\gamma_* H_N^{\mathrm{pl}},$$ where $\gamma_*$ is the constant in Theorem 1. |
| [stmt-auto-remark-1](#stmt-auto-remark-1) | remark | - | - | Remark 14* (Nature of the certificate). |

<a id="sec:intro"></a>
### The gap and the model

Laughlin’s wave function gave an explicit description of the fractional quantum Hall state at filling $1/3$ (Laughlin 1983) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Laughlin](#ref-Laughlin)]. Haldane’s spherical geometry and pseudopotentials identify a particularly simple parent Hamiltonian: every pair pays energy one in relative angular momentum one and zero in the other pair sectors (Haldane 1983) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[Haldane](#ref-Haldane)]. Its exact Laughlin zero mode is known, but a uniform lower bound on the positive spectrum requires control of all competing many-particle states. We prove this bound for the full interaction on the round sphere, resolving positively the spherical fermionic Laughlin spectral-gap conjecture.

The gap question has accompanied this model since its early development. Haldane and Rezayi’s finite-size study supplied numerical evidence and explicitly distinguished the expected hard-core gap from a formal proof (Haldane and Rezayi 1985) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[HaldaneRezayi1985FiniteSize](#ref-HaldaneRezayi1985FiniteSize)]. The density-wave variational picture of Girvin, MacDonald, and Platzman explains important features of the excitation spectrum (Girvin, MacDonald, and Platzman 1986) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[GMP](#ref-GMP)]. A variational excitation energy is an upper bound; the lower bound sought here must hold for every state orthogonal to the zero modes.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### Normalization and main results

<a id="eq:physicalH"></a>
For an integer $Q\ge1$, let $U_Q$ be the spin-$Q/2$ representation of $SU(2)$, equipped with its invariant inner product. It is the one-particle lowest Landau level on the sphere with $Q$ flux quanta. Equivalently, it is the space of homogeneous polynomials of degree $Q$ in spinor coordinates $(u,v)$. For $2\le N\le Q+1$, define $$\label{eq:physicalH}
 H_{N,Q}=\left.\sum_{1\le i<j\le N}P_{ij}^{(1)}
          \right|_{\bigwedge^N U_Q},$$ where $P_{ij}^{(1)}$ is the orthogonal projector onto pair spin $Q-1$ in tensor positions $i,j$. This is relative angular momentum one. The sum preserves the antisymmetric subspace. Each unordered pair has coefficient one, with no rescaling depending on $N$ or $Q$.

Set $H_{0,Q}=H_{1,Q}=0$, and write $$\mathcal F_Q=\bigoplus_{N=0}^{Q+1}\bigwedge^N U_Q,\qquad
 H_Q=\bigoplus_{N=0}^{Q+1}H_{N,Q},\qquad
 \gamma_*=\frac{4616733319001}{10^{14}}> \frac1{25}.$$ Our principal estimate concerns this entire Fock space.

<a id="stmt-thm-fock"></a>
<div id="thm:fock" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *For every $0<\gamma<\gamma_*$, there is an integer $Q_\gamma$ such that for every integer $Q\ge Q_\gamma$, <a id="eq:architecture"></a>$$\label{eq:architecture}
 H_Q^2\ge\gamma H_Q\qquad\text{on }\mathcal F_Q.$$ The threshold $Q_\gamma$ is independent of particle number.*

</div>

Thus the spectrum is contained in $\{0\}\cup[\gamma,\infty)$. In a particle sector without a zero mode, this statement bounds the energy away from zero; it does not assert a gap between that sector’s two lowest eigenvalues. At Laughlin filling the zero mode is unique, and we obtain the usual ground-state spectral gap.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="corollary">

**Corollary 2**. *There is an integer $N_0\ge2$ such that, for every $N\ge N_0$ and $Q=3(N-1)$, <a id="eq:main"></a>$$\label{eq:main}
 H_{N,Q}\ge\frac1{25}\bigl(I-P_{\mathrm L,N}\bigr)
 \qquad\text{on }\bigwedge^N U_Q,$$ where $P_{\mathrm L,N}$ projects onto the line spanned by $$\Psi_{\mathrm L,N}=\prod_{i<j}(u_iv_j-u_jv_i)^3.$$*

</div>

At fixed magnetic length, the sphere radius is proportional to $\sqrt Q$, so this is the two-dimensional thermodynamic limit. The thresholds in Theorem <a href="#thm:fock" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:fock">1</a> and Corollary <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">2</a> are existential. The proof does not give an explicit flux threshold or the endpoint coefficient $\gamma_*$ on finite spheres.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### Prior work

The exact ground-state structure is an established part of the theory. Besides Haldane’s spherical construction, Trugman and Kivelson obtained exact Laughlin ground states for repulsive interactions in a vanishing-range limit (Trugman and Kivelson 1985) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[TrugmanKivelson1985](#ref-TrugmanKivelson1985)]. The second-quantized algebra of pseudopotentials was developed systematically by Ortiz, Nussinov, Dukelsky, and Seidel (Ortiz et al. 2013) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[OrtizNussinovDukelskySeidel](#ref-OrtizNussinovDukelskySeidel)]. Chen and Seidel, and Mazaheri, Ortiz, Nussinov, and Seidel, gave algebraic constructions and characterizations of their zero modes (Chen and Seidel 2015; Mazaheri et al. 2015) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[ChenSeidel2015](#ref-ChenSeidel2015), [MazaheriOrtizNussinovSeidel2015](#ref-MazaheriOrtizNussinovSeidel2015)]. These results identify the zero modes that a spectral estimate must preserve. We include a short self-contained proof of the known spherical zero-mode uniqueness at the Laughlin flux in Lemma <a href="#lem:kernel" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:kernel">11</a>.

For Laughlin states on a cylinder of any fixed radius, Jansen established thermodynamic correlation limits and axial periodicity (Jansen 2012) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[Jansen2012](#ref-Jansen2012)]. Exact ground states of related finite-range orbital chains, including squeezed and matrix-product states, were constructed by Nakamura, Wang, and Bergholtz (Nakamura, Wang, and Bergholtz 2012) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[NakamuraWangBergholtz2012](#ref-NakamuraWangBergholtz2012)] and by Wang and Nakamura (Wang and Nakamura 2013) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[WangNakamura2013](#ref-WangNakamura2013)]. Nachtergaele, Warzel, and Young proved uniform gaps for a truncated fermionic pseudopotential (Nachtergaele, Warzel, and Young 2021) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[NWY](#ref-NWY)]; Warzel and Young obtained thin-torus bounds that separate bulk behavior from open-chain edge modes (Warzel and Young 2022) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[WY](#ref-WY)]. Their invariant-subspace method also treats a truncated bosonic pseudopotential (Warzel and Young 2023) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[WarzelYoung2023BulkEdge](#ref-WarzelYoung2023BulkEdge)]. The fermionic estimates already hold uniformly across particle sectors. The distinction in Theorem <a href="#thm:fock" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:fock">1</a> is the full spherical pair interaction, with its fixed projector normalization and sphere radius growing in the thermodynamic limit. The finite support used in our auxiliary comparison operators does not truncate that interaction.

Recent work gives further structural and spectral information. Lemm, Nachtergaele, Warzel, and Young derive recursions between particle sectors and compare charge and neutral gaps (Lemm et al. 2026) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[LemmNachtergaeleWarzelYoung2026Recursive](#ref-LemmNachtergaeleWarzelYoung2026Recursive)]; a uniform bound for the full pseudopotential still requires control of the model-specific ground-space overlap matrices in those criteria. Schraven and Warzel prove entanglement-gap and clustering results for Laughlin states on sufficiently thin cylinders (Schraven and Warzel 2026) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[SchravenWarzel2026IMPS](#ref-SchravenWarzel2026IMPS)]. The entanglement spectrum is distinct from the Hamiltonian spectrum considered here. Density rigidity within the family of fully correlated Laughlin states is another complementary form of incompressibility, established by Lieb, Rougerie, and Yngvason (Lieb, Rougerie, and Yngvason 2018) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[LRYRigidity](#ref-LRYRigidity)]. No density-screening assumption enters our argument.

Rougerie’s survey records the spectral-gap conjecture and its geometric formulations (Rougerie 2018--2019, Appendix A) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[Rougerie](#ref-Rougerie)]. For $N$ fermions in the planar lowest Landau level, consider the sum over unordered pairs of the orthogonal projectors onto relative polynomial degree one, each with coefficient one and with center-of-mass degree unrestricted. Section <a href="#sec:plane" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:plane">7</a> proves that its positive spectrum is bounded below by the endpoint $\gamma_*$, uniformly in particle number $N\ge2$ and total polynomial degree $L$. In particular, this proves the fermionic cubic case of the folklore spectral-gap conjecture in (Rougerie 2018--2019, Conjecture A.1) <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[Rougerie](#ref-Rougerie)], whose degree range is $L\le3N(N-1)/2$.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
#### Proof strategy

Proving positivity of $H^2-\gamma H$ is a standard route to a gap, underlying finite-size criteria (Knabe 1988) <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[Knabe](#ref-Knabe)] and methods based on positive local decompositions (Cruz et al. 2022; Rai et al. 2026) <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[Cruz](#ref-Cruz), [Rai](#ref-Rai)]. Our argument uses rotations and angular-momentum decomposition to construct such a comparison. It starts from the normal-ordered expansion of $H_Q^2$. It has two-, three-, and four-body terms. We compare them with the corresponding terms of a positive operator $$\mathcal K_Q=(2Q-1)\int_{SU(2)}
                  \sum_{\rho=1}^{7}F_\rho(g)^\dagger F_\rho(g)\,dg,$$ where each $F_\rho$ is a specified linear combination of a pair annihilator and operators that annihilate three particles and create one. Here $F_\rho(g)$ denotes rotation by $g$, and Haar measure has mass one. These auxiliary operators involve finitely many orbital modes; the Hamiltonian itself is never truncated.

Rotational symmetry reduces the comparison to fixed spin blocks. The three-body blocks have an explicit spectrum and a summable tail. For four particles, coupling the interacting pair to the different spin sectors of a second pair can produce several copies of the same total spin. Their four-particle images under exterior multiplication can be linearly dependent. For the retained blocks, seven rows of rational data establish finite inequalities in their large-flux limits. The remaining issue is to transfer those inequalities to finite spheres without an error that grows with the number of particles.

The key device is an exact diagonal change of variables for the pair coefficients. On a fixed set of orbital modes, it converts finite-flux annihilators into their limiting forms. Every perturbation is then bounded by the local pair energy. Rotation averaging turns this into $o(1)H_Q$ on the full Fock space. This relative estimate remains valid when the Gram matrices of these limiting four-particle images have kernels. A second useful observation is to apply Bessel’s inequality to all equivalent spin copies at once; this substantially reduces the four-body comparison error.

The comparisons leave a positive margin: <a id="eq:budget"></a>$$\label{eq:budget}
 H_Q^2\ge\mathcal K_Q+
       \bigl(\gamma_*-o(1)\bigr)H_Q.$$ All errors and convergence thresholds are independent of particle number. Figure <a href="#fig:transfer" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fig:transfer">1</a> isolates the passage responsible for this uniformity. A small norm error on a growing many-particle space would not suffice: it must vanish on the zero modes and be controlled by the pair energy. The fixed-mode comparison gives precisely that control before rotations distribute it over the sphere.

<figure id="fig:transfer">

<figcaption>The finite-sphere comparison preserves an error relative to the pair energy at every step. The limiting positivity is imposed only after wedging; no invertibility of its Gram matrices is required.</figcaption>
</figure>

<a id="pm-figsrc-fig-transfer"></a>
